People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Yeah… I do. A lot, actually.

I keep thinking I’m just… no good, you know? Like after I lost that job, everything kind of proved it or something. Now my relatives have to help me, and I hate that. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there.   },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

Yeah… my family notices it more than I do, I think. They say I move like I’m walking through mud, like everything takes me forever.

Even answering something simple… someone asks me a question and I just sit there. Not because I’m trying to be rude. It’s like the words are stuck somewhere. My aunt said it reminds her of this old truck we had that wouldn’t start unless you turned the key a few times… I don’t know why I thought of that.

But yeah, slower. That’s what they say.

I used to like walking by the lake, you know, just watching the water and all that. There’s this one bench where people leave crumbs for birds, though you’re probably not supposed to—anyway. And I had these little model kits I’d work on, tiny pieces, cars and stuff. Now they just sit there. I look at them and it’s like… like they belong to somebody else or something. I don’t really feel like picking them up.\n\nDoctor: How much weight would you say you've lost since you started eating less?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know the number… I haven’t weighed myself. We used to have a scale but it got shoved in a closet somewhere, I think under some old towels. But my pants are definitely looser, like I’m always pulling them up now. I just haven’t been eating much—coffee mostly, maybe bread—so I figure I’ve lost some.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, yeah. I wake up and there’s this heavy kind of empty feeling right here, like in my chest. Like before I even get up, it’s already there, like nothing good’s really waiting for me.\n\nI try to just keep my head down, you know… do what I have to do, if anything. It’s weird, my aunt used to say mornings were the best part of the day because the house was quiet, but now the quiet just feels… I don’t know. Empty.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, empty feeling?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been a while. I wanna say about five months now, maybe around there. It kind of settled in and just… stayed.\n\nI remember thinking it would pass after a week or two, like bad weather, you know? My uncle used to say clouds always move if you wait long enough, but this didn’t really move. It’s just been hanging over me for months.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're no longer able to enjoy things that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I used to like walking by the lake, just kind of watching the water and the ducks and all that. There’s this one bench I used to sit on—well, if nobody else had taken it, there was this older guy who always sat there with a radio sometimes—but now I don’t really go.\n\nAnd my little model kits, I used to spend hours on those, like planes and cars and stuff. They’re just sitting there now. I look at them and it’s like… they belong to somebody else, not me. Like I can’t get myself to care about them.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. A lot, actually.\n\nI keep thinking I’m just… no good, you know? Like after I lost that job, everything kind of proved it or something. Now my relatives have to help me, and I hate that. My cousin dropped off groceries the other day—well, it was raining and the bag got all wet, and I remember thinking I should’ve told him not to leave it by the door—but then I started thinking, why does he even have to do that for me in the first place?\n\nI replay stuff over and over. Every mistake. Things I said wrong, things I didn’t do right. It feels like I ruined everything somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling restless and unable to sit still lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my family notices it more than I do, I think. They say I move like I’m walking through mud, like everything takes me forever.\n\nEven answering something simple… someone asks me a question and I just sit there. Not because I’m trying to be rude. It’s like the words are stuck somewhere. That’s what they say.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you've lost a significant amount of weight, like more than 5% of your body weight, since this all started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I just haven’t been eating much—coffee mostly, maybe bread—so I figure I’ve lost some.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, yeah. I wake up and there’s this heavy kind of empty feeling right here, like in my chest. Like before I even get up, it’s already there, like nothing good’s really waiting for me.\n\nI try to just keep my head down, you know… do what I have to do, if anything. It’s weird, my aunt used to say mornings were the best part of the day because the house was quiet, but now the quiet just feels… I don’t know. Empty.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, empty feeling?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been a while. I wanna say about five months now, maybe around there. It kind of settled in and just… stayed.\n\nI remember thinking it would pass after a week or two, like bad weather, you know? My uncle used to say clouds always move if you wait long enough, but this didn’t really move. It’s just been hanging over me for months.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're no longer able to enjoy things that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I used to like walking by the lake, just kind of watching the water and the ducks and all that. There’s this one bench I used to sit on—well, if nobody else had taken it, there was this older guy who always sat there with a radio sometimes—but now I don’t really go.\n\nAnd my little model kits, I used to spend hours on those, like planes and cars and stuff. They’re just sitting there now. I look at them and it’s like… they belong to somebody else, not me. Like I can’t get myself to care about them.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. A lot, actually.\n\nI keep thinking I’m just… no good, you know? Like after I lost that job, everything kind of proved it or something. Now my relatives have to help me, and I hate that. My cousin dropped off groceries the other day—well, it was raining and the bag got all wet, and I remember thinking I should’ve told him not to leave it by the door—but then I started thinking, why does he even have to do that for me in the first place?\n\nI replay stuff over and over. Every mistake. Things I said wrong, things I didn’t do right. It feels like I ruined everything somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling restless and unable to sit still lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my family notices it more than I do, I think. They say I move like I’m walking through mud, like everything takes me forever.\n\nEven answering something simple… someone asks me a question and I just sit there. Not because I’m trying to be rude. It’s like the words are stuck somewhere. My aunt said it reminds her of this old truck we had that wouldn’t start unless you turned the key a few times… I don’t know why I thought of that.\n\nBut yeah, slower. That’s what they say.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you've lost a significant amount of weight, like more than 5% of your body weight, since this all started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t have a scale that’s worth anything—it's one of those old bathroom ones that sticks, you know, you tap it and the needle jumps around—but my pants are loose now. Like they’re hanging off me more than they used to.\n\nI just… I hardly eat right anymore. Some days it’s only coffee, and maybe a piece of bread if it’s there. I’ll open the cupboard and just stand there, and then I end up closing it again. So, yeah, I guess I’ve lost weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to live or hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know how to answer that cleanly.\n\nSometimes I get real tired of waking up and having to keep doing this, if that’s what you mean. But hurting myself… I don’t know. My mind kind of runs off before I can pin it down. Like I’ll be sitting there thinking about bills, and then I’m thinking about how the kitchen chair wobbles because one leg is shorter, and then I forget what I was even thinking.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. It’s not something I’ve got a clear answer for right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last two weeks where you felt better for a couple of days?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I wish I could say yes, but most mornings I wake up and it’s just this heavy, empty feeling right here in my chest. Like before I even get my feet on the floor, it already feels like nothing good is waiting for me.\n\nSometimes the light comes through the window funny, and I think I should fix that curtain because it hangs crooked—my mom used to get mad about crooked curtains—but then I just keep my head down and don’t do much with it. So… better for a couple days? No, not really.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days recently where you felt noticeably better than the others?\n\nPatient: No… not noticeably. Most days I wake up and it’s just that same heavy, empty feeling in my chest, like… before anything’s even happened, I already feel like nothing good’s waiting for me.\n\nI’ll think maybe if I get up and open the window or straighten something out—there’s this stack of mail on the table that keeps sliding sideways—but then I just keep my head down. So, no, I don’t think I’ve had a day that really felt better than the others.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: I used to, yeah… I used to like walking down by the lake. There’s this one spot where the ducks all crowd around like they own the place, and the railing’s kind of rusty—been that way forever, I think.\n\nAnd I had these little model kits at home, cars and things. I used to sit with them for hours. Now they just sit there on the table, still in the boxes mostly, and I look at them like… I don’t know, like they belong to somebody else. I don’t really look forward to them now.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that still bring you a bit of joy or happiness?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really joy, no. I mean, I used to like walking by the lake, like I said, where the ducks all bunch up by the railing—people feed them even though there’s that sign, and the sign’s been faded for years.\n\nAnd my little model kits… I used to get kind of happy sitting with those, lining up the pieces, painting tiny parts. Now they just sit there in the boxes, and I look at them like they belong to somebody else. So… small things, maybe they should, but they don’t really feel like much right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days where you felt completely back to your normal self?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not back to normal.\n\nMost days I wake up and it’s already there, that heavy, empty feeling in my chest, like the day’s decided before I even put my feet on the floor. Like nothing good is waiting for me. I’ll just keep my head down, you know… even if the room’s a mess or there’s stuff I should do. So, no, I haven’t had a day where I felt like myself again.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not really… I mean, like I said, I used to like walking down by the lake. There’s that path where the ducks all crowd around, and sometimes people leave bread even though you’re not supposed to. I’d go there and it used to feel like something, you know?

And the model kits… I’ve still got them. Little cars, mostly. They’re just sitting there now, still in the boxes, or half-started. I look at them and it’s like they belong to somebody else. I don’t really get pleasure from them anymore.

I used to like walking by the lake, you know, just watching the water and all that. There’s this one bench where people leave crumbs for birds, though you’re probably not supposed to—anyway. And I had these little model kits I’d work on, tiny pieces, cars and stuff. Now they just sit there. I look at them and it’s like… like they belong to somebody else or something. I don’t really feel like picking them up.\n\nDoctor: How much weight would you say you've lost since you started eating less?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know the number… I haven’t weighed myself. We used to have a scale but it got shoved in a closet somewhere, I think under some old towels. But my pants are definitely looser, like I’m always pulling them up now. I just haven’t been eating much—coffee mostly, maybe bread—so I figure I’ve lost some.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, yeah. I wake up and there’s this heavy kind of empty feeling right here, like in my chest. Like before I even get up, it’s already there, like nothing good’s really waiting for me.\n\nI try to just keep my head down, you know… do what I have to do, if anything. It’s weird, my aunt used to say mornings were the best part of the day because the house was quiet, but now the quiet just feels… I don’t know. Empty.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, empty feeling?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been a while. I wanna say about five months now, maybe around there. It kind of settled in and just… stayed.\n\nI remember thinking it would pass after a week or two, like bad weather, you know? My uncle used to say clouds always move if you wait long enough, but this didn’t really move. It’s just been hanging over me for months.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're no longer able to enjoy things that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I used to like walking by the lake, just kind of watching the water and the ducks and all that. There’s this one bench I used to sit on—well, if nobody else had taken it, there was this older guy who always sat there with a radio sometimes—but now I don’t really go.\n\nAnd my little model kits, I used to spend hours on those, like planes and cars and stuff. They’re just sitting there now. I look at them and it’s like… they belong to somebody else, not me. Like I can’t get myself to care about them.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. A lot, actually.\n\nI keep thinking I’m just… no good, you know? Like after I lost that job, everything kind of proved it or something. Now my relatives have to help me, and I hate that. My cousin dropped off groceries the other day—well, it was raining and the bag got all wet, and I remember thinking I should’ve told him not to leave it by the door—but then I started thinking, why does he even have to do that for me in the first place?\n\nI replay stuff over and over. Every mistake. Things I said wrong, things I didn’t do right. It feels like I ruined everything somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling restless and unable to sit still lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my family notices it more than I do, I think. It’s like the words are stuck somewhere. I don’t have a scale that’s worth anything—it's one of those old bathroom ones that sticks, you know, you tap it and the needle jumps around—but my pants are loose now. I’ll open the cupboard and just stand there, and then I end up closing it again. It’s not something I’ve got a clear answer for right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last two weeks where you felt better for a couple of days?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I wish I could say yes, but most mornings I wake up and it’s just this heavy, empty feeling right here in my chest. Like before I even get my feet on the floor, it already feels like nothing good is waiting for me.\n\nSometimes the light comes through the window funny, and I think I should fix that curtain because it hangs crooked—my mom used to get mad about crooked curtains—but then I just keep my head down and don’t do much with it. So… small things, maybe they should, but they don’t really feel like much right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days where you felt completely back to your normal self?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not back to normal.\n\nMost days I wake up and it’s already there, that heavy, empty feeling in my chest, like the day’s decided before I even put my feet on the floor. Like nothing good is waiting for me. I’ll just keep my head down, you know… even if the room’s a mess or there’s stuff I should do. So, no, I haven’t had a day where I felt like myself again.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, like I said, I used to like walking down by the lake. There’s that path where the ducks all crowd around, and sometimes people leave bread even though you’re not supposed to. I’d go there and it used to feel like something, you know?\n\nAnd the model kits… I’ve still got them. Little cars, mostly. They’re just sitting there now, still in the boxes, or half-started. I look at them and it’s like they belong to somebody else. I don’t really get pleasure from them anymore.\n\n[Last Question]\nHow many months have you been feeling this way, with the heavy, empty feeling and loss of interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. About… five months, I think. It kind of started before I really noticed it was sticking around, if that makes sense.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Uh, yeah… I guess what made me come in is I’m not really eating right anymore. Some days it’s just coffee, maybe a piece of bread if it’s there. And I know that sounds silly, like everyone skips meals, my uncle used to do that when he was working nights—well, I’m not working, but… anyway, my pants are getting real loose on me, hanging off more than they should.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in the things you enjoy doing or used to look forward to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I used to like walking by the lake, you know, just watching the water and all that. There’s this one bench where people leave crumbs for birds, though you’re probably not supposed to—anyway. And I had these little model kits I’d work on, tiny pieces, cars and stuff. Now they just sit there. I look at them and it’s like… like they belong to somebody else or something. I don’t really feel like picking them up.\n\nDoctor: How much weight would you say you've lost since you started eating less?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know the number… I haven’t weighed myself. We used to have a scale but it got shoved in a closet somewhere, I think under some old towels. But my pants are definitely looser, like I’m always pulling them up now. I just haven’t been eating much—coffee mostly, maybe bread—so I figure I’ve lost some.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, yeah. I wake up and there’s this heavy kind of empty feeling right here, like in my chest. Like before I even get up, it’s already there, like nothing good’s really waiting for me.\n\nI try to just keep my head down, you know… do what I have to do, if anything. It’s weird, my aunt used to say mornings were the best part of the day because the house was quiet, but now the quiet just feels… I don’t know. Empty.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, empty feeling?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been a while. I wanna say about five months now, maybe around there. It kind of settled in and just… stayed.\n\nI remember thinking it would pass after a week or two, like bad weather, you know? My uncle used to say clouds always move if you wait long enough, but this didn’t really move. It’s just been hanging over me for months.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're no longer able to enjoy things that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I used to like walking by the lake, just kind of watching the water and the ducks and all that. There’s this one bench I used to sit on—well, if nobody else had taken it, there was this older guy who always sat there with a radio sometimes—but now I don’t really go.\n\nAnd my little model kits, I used to spend hours on those, like planes and cars and stuff. They’re just sitting there now. I look at them and it’s like… they belong to somebody else, not me. Like I can’t get myself to care about them.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. A lot, actually.\n\nI keep thinking I’m just… no good, you know? Like after I lost that job, everything kind of proved it or something. Now my relatives have to help me, and I hate that. My cousin dropped off groceries the other day—well, it was raining and the bag got all wet, and I remember thinking I should’ve told him not to leave it by the door—but then I started thinking, why does he even have to do that for me in the first place?\n\nI replay stuff over and over. Every mistake. Things I said wrong, things I didn’t do right. It feels like I ruined everything somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling restless and unable to sit still lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my family notices it more than I do, I think. They say I move like I’m walking through mud, like everything takes me forever.\n\nEven answering something simple… someone asks me a question and I just sit there. Not because I’m trying to be rude. It’s like the words are stuck somewhere. My aunt said it reminds her of this old truck we had that wouldn’t start unless you turned the key a few times… I don’t know why I thought of that.\n\nBut yeah, slower. That’s what they say.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you've lost a significant amount of weight, like more than 5% of your body weight, since this all started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t have a scale that’s worth anything—it's one of those old bathroom ones that sticks, you know, you tap it and the needle jumps around—but my pants are loose now. Like they’re hanging off me more than they used to.\n\nI just… I hardly eat right anymore. Some days it’s only coffee, and maybe a piece of bread if it’s there. I’ll open the cupboard and just stand there, and then I end up closing it again. So, yeah, I guess I’ve lost weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to live or hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know how to answer that cleanly.\n\nSometimes I get real tired of waking up and having to keep doing this, if that’s what you mean. But hurting myself… I don’t know. My mind kind of runs off before I can pin it down. Like I’ll be sitting there thinking about bills, and then I’m thinking about how the kitchen chair wobbles because one leg is shorter, and then I forget what I was even thinking.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. It’s not something I’ve got a clear answer for right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last two weeks where you felt better for a couple of days?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I wish I could say yes, but most mornings I wake up and it’s just this heavy, empty feeling right here in my chest. Like before I even get my feet on the floor, it already feels like nothing good is waiting for me.\n\nSometimes the light comes through the window funny, and I think I should fix that curtain because it hangs crooked—my mom used to get mad about crooked curtains—but then I just keep my head down and don’t do much with it. So… better for a couple days? No, not really.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days recently where you felt noticeably better than the others?\n\nPatient: No… not noticeably. Most days I wake up and it’s just that same heavy, empty feeling in my chest, like… before anything’s even happened, I already feel like nothing good’s waiting for me.\n\nI’ll think maybe if I get up and open the window or straighten something out—there’s this stack of mail on the table that keeps sliding sideways—but then I just keep my head down. So, no, I don’t think I’ve had a day that really felt better than the others.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: I used to, yeah… I used to like walking down by the lake. I used to like walking by the lake, you know, just watching the water and all that. There’s this one bench where people leave crumbs for birds, though you’re probably not supposed to—anyway. And I had these little model kits I’d work on, tiny pieces, cars and stuff. Now they just sit there. I look at them and it’s like… like they belong to somebody else or something. I don’t really feel like picking them up.\n\nDoctor: How much weight would you say you've lost since you started eating less?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know the number… I haven’t weighed myself. We used to have a scale but it got shoved in a closet somewhere, I think under some old towels. But my pants are definitely looser, like I’m always pulling them up now. I just haven’t been eating much—coffee mostly, maybe bread—so I figure I’ve lost some.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, yeah. I wake up and there’s this heavy kind of empty feeling right here, like in my chest. Like before I even get up, it’s already there, like nothing good’s really waiting for me.\n\nI try to just keep my head down, you know… do what I have to do, if anything. It’s weird, my aunt used to say mornings were the best part of the day because the house was quiet, but now the quiet just feels… I don’t know. Empty.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, empty feeling?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been a while. I wanna say about five months now, maybe around there. It kind of settled in and just… stayed.\n\nI remember thinking it would pass after a week or two, like bad weather, you know? My uncle used to say clouds always move if you wait long enough, but this didn’t really move. It’s just been hanging over me for months.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're no longer able to enjoy things that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I used to like walking by the lake, just kind of watching the water and the ducks and all that. There’s this one bench I used to sit on—well, if nobody else had taken it, there was this older guy who always sat there with a radio sometimes—but now I don’t really go.\n\nAnd my little model kits, I used to spend hours on those, like planes and cars and stuff. They’re just sitting there now. I look at them and it’s like… they belong to somebody else, not me. Like I can’t get myself to care about them.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. A lot, actually.\n\nI keep thinking I’m just… no good, you know? Like after I lost that job, everything kind of proved it or something. Now my relatives have to help me, and I hate that. My cousin dropped off groceries the other day—well, it was raining and the bag got all wet, and I remember thinking I should’ve told him not to leave it by the door—but then I started thinking, why does he even have to do that for me in the first place?\n\nI replay stuff over and over. Every mistake. Things I said wrong, things I didn’t do right. It feels like I ruined everything somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling restless and unable to sit still lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my family notices it more than I do, I think. They say I move like I’m walking through mud, like everything takes me forever.\n\nEven answering something simple… someone asks me a question and I just sit there. Not because I’m trying to be rude. It’s like the words are stuck somewhere. My aunt said it reminds her of this old truck we had that wouldn’t start unless you turned the key a few times… I don’t know why I thought of that.\n\nBut yeah, slower. That’s what they say.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you've lost a significant amount of weight, like more than 5% of your body weight, since this all started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t have a scale that’s worth anything—it's one of those old bathroom ones that sticks, you know, you tap it and the needle jumps around—but my pants are loose now. Like they’re hanging off me more than they used to.\n\nI just… I hardly eat right anymore. Some days it’s only coffee, and maybe a piece of bread if it’s there. I’ll open the cupboard and just stand there, and then I end up closing it again. So, yeah, I guess I’ve lost weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to live or hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know how to answer that cleanly.\n\nSometimes I get real tired of waking up and having to keep doing this, if that’s what you mean. But hurting myself… I don’t know. My mind kind of runs off before I can pin it down. Like I’ll be sitting there thinking about bills, and then I’m thinking about how the kitchen chair wobbles because one leg is shorter, and then I forget what I was even thinking.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. It’s not something I’ve got a clear answer for right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last two weeks where you felt better for a couple of days?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I wish I could say yes, but most mornings I wake up and it’s just this heavy, empty feeling right here in my chest. Like before I even get my feet on the floor, it already feels like nothing good is waiting for me.\n\nSometimes the light comes through the window funny, and I think I should fix that curtain because it hangs crooked—my mom used to get mad about crooked curtains—but then I just keep my head down and don’t do much with it. So… better for a couple days? No, not really.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days recently where you felt noticeably better than the others?\n\nPatient: No… not noticeably. Most days I wake up and it’s just that same heavy, empty feeling in my chest, like… before anything’s even happened, I already feel like nothing good’s waiting for me.\n\nI’ll think maybe if I get up and open the window or straighten something out—there’s this stack of mail on the table that keeps sliding sideways—but then I just keep my head down. So, no, I don’t think I’ve had a day that really felt better than the others.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: I used to, yeah… I used to like walking down by the lake. There’s this one spot where the ducks all crowd around like they own the place, and the railing’s kind of rusty—been that way forever, I think.\n\nAnd I had these little model kits at home, cars and things. I used to sit with them for hours. Now they just sit there on the table, still in the boxes mostly, and I look at them like… I don’t know, like they belong to somebody else. I don’t really look forward to them now.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that still bring you a bit of joy or happiness?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really joy, no. I mean, I used to like walking by the lake, like I said, where the ducks all bunch up by the railing—people feed them even though there’s that sign, and the sign’s been faded for years.\n\nAnd my little model kits… I used to get kind of happy sitting with those, lining up the pieces, painting tiny parts. Now they just sit there in the boxes, and I look at them like they belong to somebody else. So… small things, maybe they should, but they don’t really feel like much right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days where you felt completely back to your normal self?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not back to normal.\n\nMost days I wake up and it’s already there, that heavy, empty feeling in my chest, like the day’s decided before I even put my feet on the floor. Like nothing good is waiting for me. I’ll just keep my head down, you know… even if the room’s a mess or there’s stuff I should do. I’ll open the cupboard and just stand there, and then I end up closing it again. I just haven’t been eating much—coffee mostly, maybe bread—so I figure I’ve lost some.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, yeah. I wake up and there’s this heavy kind of empty feeling right here, like in my chest. Like before I even get up, it’s already there, like nothing good’s really waiting for me.\n\nI try to just keep my head down, you know… do what I have to do, if anything. It’s weird, my aunt used to say mornings were the best part of the day because the house was quiet, but now the quiet just feels… I don’t know. Empty.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, empty feeling?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been a while. I wanna say about five months now, maybe around there. It kind of settled in and just… stayed.\n\nI remember thinking it would pass after a week or two, like bad weather, you know? My uncle used to say clouds always move if you wait long enough, but this didn’t really move. It’s just been hanging over me for months.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're no longer able to enjoy things that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I used to like walking by the lake, just kind of watching the water and the ducks and all that. There’s this one bench I used to sit on—well, if nobody else had taken it, there was this older guy who always sat there with a radio sometimes—but now I don’t really go.\n\nAnd my little model kits, I used to spend hours on those, like planes and cars and stuff. They’re just sitting there now. I look at them and it’s like… they belong to somebody else, not me. Like I can’t get myself to care about them.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. A lot, actually.\n\nI keep thinking I’m just… no good, you know? Like after I lost that job, everything kind of proved it or something. Now my relatives have to help me, and I hate that. My cousin dropped off groceries the other day—well, it was raining and the bag got all wet, and I remember thinking I should’ve told him not to leave it by the door—but then I started thinking, why does he even have to do that for me in the first place?\n\nI replay stuff over and over. Every mistake. Things I said wrong, things I didn’t do right. It feels like I ruined everything somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling restless and unable to sit still lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my family notices it more than I do, I think. They say I move like I’m walking through mud, like everything takes me forever.\n\nEven answering something simple… someone asks me a question and I just sit there. Not because I’m trying to be rude. It’s like the words are stuck somewhere. My aunt said it reminds her of this old truck we had that wouldn’t start unless you turned the key a few times… I don’t know why I thought of that.\n\nBut yeah, slower. That’s what they say.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you've lost a significant amount of weight, like more than 5% of your body weight, since this all started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t have a scale that’s worth anything—it's one of those old bathroom ones that sticks, you know, you tap it and the needle jumps around—but my pants are loose now. Like they’re hanging off me more than they used to.\n\nI just… I hardly eat right anymore. Some days it’s only coffee, and maybe a piece of bread if it’s there. I’ll open the cupboard and just stand there, and then I end up closing it again. So, yeah, I guess I’ve lost weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to live or hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know how to answer that cleanly.\n\nSometimes I get real tired of waking up and having to keep doing this, if that’s what you mean. But hurting myself… I don’t know. My mind kind of runs off before I can pin it down. Like I’ll be sitting there thinking about bills, and then I’m thinking about how the kitchen chair wobbles because one leg is shorter, and then I forget what I was even thinking.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. It’s not something I’ve got a clear answer for right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last two weeks where you felt better for a couple of days?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I wish I could say yes, but most mornings I wake up and it’s just this heavy, empty feeling right here in my chest. Like before I even get my feet on the floor, it already feels like nothing good is waiting for me.\n\nSometimes the light comes through the window funny, and I think I should fix that curtain because it hangs crooked—my mom used to get mad about crooked curtains—but then I just keep my head down and don’t do much with it. So… better for a couple days? No, not really.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days recently where you felt noticeably better than the others?\n\nPatient: No… not noticeably. Most days I wake up and it’s just that same heavy, empty feeling in my chest, like… before anything’s even happened, I already feel like nothing good’s waiting for me.\n\nI’ll think maybe if I get up and open the window or straighten something out—there’s this stack of mail on the table that keeps sliding sideways—but then I just keep my head down. So, no, I don’t think I’ve had a day that really felt better than the others.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: I used to, yeah… I used to like walking down by the lake. There’s this one spot where the ducks all crowd around like they own the place, and the railing’s kind of rusty—been that way forever, I think.\n\nAnd I had these little model kits at home, cars and things. I used to sit with them for hours. Now they just sit there on the table, still in the boxes mostly, and I look at them like… I don’t know, like they belong to somebody else. I don’t really look forward to them now.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that still bring you a bit of joy or happiness?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really joy, no. I mean, I used to like walking by the lake, like I said, where the ducks all bunch up by the railing—people feed them even though there’s that sign, and the sign’s been faded for years.\n\nAnd my little model kits… I used to get kind of happy sitting with those, lining up the pieces, painting tiny parts. Now they just sit there in the boxes, and I look at them like they belong to somebody else. So… small things, maybe they should, but they don’t really feel like much right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days where you felt completely back to your normal self?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not back to normal.\n\nMost days I wake up and it’s already there, that heavy, empty feeling in my chest, like the day’s decided before I even put my feet on the floor. Like nothing good is waiting for me. I’ll just keep my head down, you know… even if the room’s a mess or there’s stuff I should do. So, no, I haven’t had a day where I felt like myself again.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, like I said, I used to like walking down by the lake. There’s that path where the ducks all crowd around, and sometimes people leave bread even though you’re not supposed to. I’d go there and it used to feel like something, you know?\n\nAnd the model kits… I’ve still got them. Little cars, mostly. They’re just sitting there now, still in the boxes, or half-started. I look at them and it’s like they belong to somebody else. I don’t really get pleasure from them anymore.\n\nDoctor: How many months have you been feeling this way, with the heavy, empty feeling and loss of interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: About… five months, I think. It kind of started before I really noticed it was sticking around, if that makes sense.\n\nI remember because there was still that cold weather, and the lake path had all those puddles that freeze at the edges, and I kept telling myself I’d feel better when it warmed up. But then it just… didn’t lift. So yeah, this has been hanging over me for about five months now.\n\nDoctor: Is this heavy, empty feeling you have most of the time, like throughout the day, or does it come and go?\n\nPatient: It’s… most of the time, yeah. I wake up and it’s already there, that heavy empty feeling in my chest, like nothing good is waiting for me.\n\nSometimes I’ll get distracted for a minute, like if there’s a noise outside or I’m looking for something I misplaced—my keys are always ending up in weird places—but then it’s right back. It’s like I just want to keep my head down and get through the day, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any two-week period in the last five months where you didn't feel this heavy, empty feeling and you were interested in things like you used to be?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. It’s been hanging over me pretty much this whole five months.\n\nI keep trying to think if there was a stretch where it lifted, like maybe when the weather changed or when I thought I might get back to those model cars, but… no. Maybe a moment here or there where I wasn’t paying attention to it, but not two weeks. Not where I felt like myself again.\n\nDoctor: Have you had at least two weeks in the past five months where you didn't feel depressed or uninterested in activities?\n\nPatient: No… not really. I mean, this whole spell has been hanging over me for about five months now.\n\nI’ll think, “Maybe there was a better week,” but then I remember I was still dragging myself around, same as before. Like I kept waiting for it to pass, the way bad weather passes, but it didn’t. Not for two weeks, no.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
